**"Gurudeva, give to this servant just one drop of mercy. I am lower than a blade of grass. Give me all help. Give me strength. Let me be as you are, without desires or aspirations.**

*sakale sammaṇ, korite śakati,*  
*deho' nātha! yathā yatha*  
*tabe to' gāibo, harināma-sukhe,*  
*aparādha habe hata*

**"I offer to you all respects, for thus I may have the energy to know you correctly. Then, by chanting the holy name in great ecstasy, all my offenses will cease.**

*kabe heno kṛpā, lobhiyā e jana,*  
*kṛtārtha hoibe, nātha!*  
*śakti-buddhi-hīn, āmi ati dīn,*  
*koro' more ātma-sātha*

**"When will such mercy fall to this one who is weak and devoid of intelligence? Allow me to be with you.**

*yogyatā-vicāre, kichu nāhi pāi,*  
*tomāra karuṇā-sāra*  
*karuṇā nā hoile, kāṅdiyā kāṅdiyā,*  
*prāṇa nā rākhibo āra*

**"If you examine me, you will find no good qualities. Your mercy is all that I am made of. If you are not merciful unto me, I can only weep, and I will not be able to maintain my life."**

Prostrating ourselves at your lotus feet, dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, we pray for your unlimited mercy to help us become eligible for being your servants. Carrying the mercy of Kṛṣṇa as a rain cloud carries rains, you are bestowing his mercy upon anyone and everyone, just as the rain cloud pours water. The cloud does not consider whether it pours water upon forests, rocks or lakes, and similarly you do not consider upon whom you will bestow your mercy. Although Kṛṣṇa advised in B*hagavad-gītā* (18.67) that "this confidential knowledge may not be explained to those who are not austere, or devoted, or engaged in devotional service, nor to one who is envious of Me," you are more merciful than Kṛṣṇa and therefore you kindly explain to us the science of Kṛṣṇa consciousness although we are not austere, not devoted, not sincerely engaged in devotional service and we are envious of Kṛṣṇa. You are like a touchstone, which can transform iron into gold by touching it. By your kindness you have touched us with the vibration of Kṛṣṇa's holy name. Unfortunately we are so fallen that we do not like to remain in touch with you constantly and so we are foolishly hampering our own transformation. When will the day come when your words become our life and soul?

As you have explained in *Caitanya-caritāmṛta,* in the absence of the spiritual master his words are the pride of his disciples. When will the day come?

Gurudeva, give to these servants just one drop of mercy. We are lower than blades of grass. Give us strength. On this holy event of Vyāsa-Pūjā we are supposed to make an offering unto your lotus feet, but we are coming as beggars. Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda, do not kick us away. Our only wish is to have our consciousness purified by the words emanating from your lotus mouth. Give us the shade of your lotus feet.

## Guyana

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

We beg to offer our prostrated obeisances in the dust of your lotus feet.

By your mercy Lord Caitanya's message is reaching every town and village of every country. Your fame is spread all over the three worlds. Yet you never claim any credit for yourself but you always transfer all the honor to your Gurudeva.

Our only hope for advancing in love of God is to be engaged in loving service to your humble self because by such service only can we achieve the mercy or Kṛṣṇa. You are so kindly giving the shade of your lotus feet to each and every one of us, indeed to all the conditioned souls.

In the *Bhagavad-gītā* Kṛṣṇa says, "Always think of Me and become My devotee. Worship me and offer your homage unto Me. Thus you will come to me without fail." On this blessed day of your merciful appearance on this planet and Kṛṣṇa's manifested representative, we repeatedly worship and offer our homage unto you, fervently praying to be always engaged in thinking of how merciful you are to the conditioned souls and how especially fortunate we are to be accepted as disciples of an unalloyed devotee of Kṛṣṇa as yourself.

We beg to remain always in the service of your lotus feet.

*Your Guyana devotees,*

Bhūtādi dāsa / Bhakta Rajcoomar / Bhaktin Tulsidai / Bhaktin Joan / Bhaktin Savitri / Phulmatri Prabhu / Sureśa Candra Prabhu

## Honolulu

All victories and glories to Oṁ Viṣṇupāda Paramahaṁsa Parivrājakācārya 108 Śrī Śrīmān A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, our beloved spiritual master. You are translating the cream of the Vedic literatures in so many languages throughout all parts of the world so authoritatively and exquisitely that you are single-handedly destroying the voidism and impersonalism that has the world in it's stringent clutches.

We think that we have met Your grace by the will or providence, and thus we accept you as captain of the ship (*karṇa-dhāra*) for those who desire to cross the dangerous ocean of Kali, which destroys all the good qualities of a human being. (*Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* 1.1.22)

The dust of your lotus feet touching our heads converts us from ravaging zombies (*jīvāñchava*) to respectable human beings. This dust is extended in the form of your perfect instruction. This instruction is the breeze that inflates the sails of our human boat, allowing us to ply forward, across the turpid waters of the evil Kali, toward the safe and sublime shore of the spiritual sky.

Mild Hawaiian spring breezes wafted through the temple room windows. The sense of peace and satisfaction was due to the presence if the Pañca-tattva in their full glory, dressed in the opulence of multicolored silk embroidered with glittering silver sequins, some square, some star-shaped, some round. You sat placidly, dearmost Śrīla Prabhupāda, emanating deep yet comforting knowledge; the regal purple background of the plush *vyāsāsana* providing an exemplary picture of your actual stature.

"We are interested in just one moon. I am looking for one moon. There are so many stars in the sky, but the light from just one moon is of so much more value. We are looking for just one moon."

You had said this before. Such repetition is glorious, only adding to the effect. You are the soothing, cooling personified moonshine of Lord Nityānanda. The impersonalists are so much fond of the dazzling *brahma-jyotir* because this *brahma-jyotir* is the effulgence of the transcendental body of the Supreme Personality of Godhead. Everyone is looking for Kṛṣṇa. Sunlight is a direct reflection of this *brahma-jyotir.* Moonlight is the direct reflection of sunshine. The predominating deity of the moon controls everyone's mind, and in the presence of soothing moonshine, everyone feels freedom and relief from the pangs of material existence. You are the pure reflection, the empowered representative of Lord Nityānanda Rāma, the original spiritual master. Thus your realized words expertly cut through the darkness of our *avidya,* our ignorance. Although we know that we are implicated when we hear and read them, if we are sincere, we do not feel anxiety but soothing relief. Such is the effect of the transcendental moon which brightens everything amidst the age of darkness. Ignorance becomes dissipated and destroyed.

Now you want that there be just even one more moon. This you are asking of us. Full knowledge is there in all living entities but, caught from time immemorial in the clutches of the bewildering energy called *māyā,* it does not manifest—or sometimes manifests only minutely. Thus so much darkness, some scattered stars, but you are looking for just one moon.

Hawaii is the best locale in the world to practice spiritual life. One can live on as little as two coconut dobs a day. This you have stated. Hawaii is the best place to absorb oneself in material happiness also. But pouring water on a fire while trying to ignite it is the strategy of the deluded. For one who is trying to realize spiritual ecstasy but has not abandoned material hankerings, Hawaii is not the best place.

We have seen you prosper here, and display all the transcendental qualities of the pure devotee. Thus you have allowed us to prosper also. By Kṛṣṇa's grace, ISKCON—Honolulu has been presented the opportunity to afford you an opulent mansion located in one of the best valleys of the city, a valley noted for it's many churches and low crime rate. There are beautiful mango trees on and near the property. You have written to us, "and everyone knows how fond I am of mangos." The shade of the immense banyan tree in the backyard provides strength and vitality. While walking atop the open veranda which adjoins your room and overlooks a circular hedge of more than thirty *tulasī* trees, you once glanced toward the vegetable garden in the near north. Seeing the devotee working there, bare-chested and basking in the morning sun, you said to him from above, "be blessed."

But of all your prosperity, the most invigorating display of your opulence here was in the supremely important matter of your writing. Your pace was phenomenal, never before seen. Leaving almost all of the public lecturing to your disciples, you would speak from the *vyāsāsana* only on Sunday evenings, presenting the information that after death one may, on the planet of the plutonic king, relish embracing the red-hot form of a woman in he is so much immersed in that activity during life.

So much of the rest of the time you would translate. Later we would read the description of the hellish planets in the Fifth Canto of *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and, at the culmination of the work, also experience your unprecedented acknowledgement, "Completed in the Honolulu temple of the Pañca-tattva, June 5, 1975." You would sit in front of the deity and tell us that this mixing business should not go on. It is not the policy of the moonlike devotees.

In Hawaii it has been all too prevalent. So many have come; so many have gone. This we have seen.

The platform of pure devotional service is detailed in the Twelfth Chapter of the *Bhagavad-gītā.* The very first quality of a pure devotee is listed there. *Adveṣṭā:* nonenvious.

There are many symptoms of nescience; there are many contradictions labored under by the conditioned soul. Just like of one has stomach problems due to malfunctioning of his nerves in that area. He may take so much trouble to eat correct and properly combined foods along with balanced exercise and rest. But if the nerves leading from the brain to the stomach are pinched by a sublaxation of the vertebrae, what is the use of all such remedies? He must go to a chiropractor and have his spinal column put back in order. Then everything will go on nicely.

Similarly, we have to get to the root of our contamination, not just try to neutralize the symptoms, leaving the dirt underneath the rug. This is what you are giving us with this *bhakti-yoga* system. The other *yoga* or so-called *yoga* methodologies on this planet today don't come to grips with the root reason of our undesirable existence here. Almost all of their constituents are impersonalists and voidists. Although they might be able to have their relative truths securely established within, and although they are able to gloss over some of the symptoms of their contamination, thus manifesting some seemingly good qualities which will flicker and fail in the clutch, until they come to the platform of accepting the personal existence of the Supreme Truth and acting accordingly, their initial contamination remains. The system of devotional service you are so mercifully extending to us might not contain as many artificial austerities as some of the other more popular methods and concoctions, but only a few fortunate and rare souls will actually take this medicine. It is a medicine that cannot be compromised. You relentlessly rip away at our illusions, revealing things as they are. Not everyone really wants to know what they need to know. Some will come, and some will go.

You are teaching us what we have forgotten. We are originally all pure spiritual souls. But our eternal function is not God, but *dāsa, dāsa, dāsa,* loving servitorship to God. Subordinate. We fell from that position due to our original sin. The Christians also have this concept of original sin, but when you ask them to elucidate matters, they cannot. This is because their system also allows that despicable leeway to ultimately balk. The reason for our falling from the spiritual world was due to our original envy and pride toward Lord Kṛṣṇa. We thought, "why do I have to serve Kṛṣṇa, I am just as good as Kṛṣṇa." Thus we were sent down to get covered over again and again in the masquerade ball called *saṁsāra,* the excruciating cycle of birth and death. Our original light has now become covered by so many curtains, all linked to one another very systematically and scientifically. You are forcing us, your disciples, to realize and remove that original curtain so that we won't be forced to entangle ourselves only with subsequent symptoms. They will be lifted automatically, and thus by your grace, our moon will burst forth in full glory.

You are the eternal well-wisher of all living entities. Your mercy cannot be fathomed. You have taken the most fallen and have rejuvenated their God-given powers of intelligence and strength. But, by the eternal presence of minute independence, the *jīva* servitor can elect to turn his face from you, and futily try to imitate the eternal enjoyer. Thus for the soul still affected by the coverings of gross and subtle bodies, devotional service is like a razor's edge. Some are following strictly and cleaning their faces so meticulously perfect, while others are suffering so many knicks and cuts. The most damaging misuse of this potency you are bestowing is *guru-aparādha* (*hāthī-māttā,* the elephant offense) and *vaiṣṇava-aparādha,* offense to your devotees. These things ultimately surface because of the original sinful seed: envy.

You have written about this in your purport to verse of Śrīla Rūpa Gosvāmī's *Upadeśāmṛta* (*The Nectar of Instruction*) as follows:

"This Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is a transcendental science, and there is no room for jealousy. As soon as one becomes envious, he falls from the platform of *paramahaṁsa.* If we consider the bodily defects of a Vaiṣṇava, we should understand that we are committing an offense at the lotus feet of the Vaiṣṇava, which is very serious. For a neophyte especially, considering a pure devotee from a material point of view is very injurious. One should try to see the internal features and understand how he is engaged in the transcendental loving service of the Lord. Those who think that Kṛṣṇa consciousness is limited to a certain section of people or devotees are generally prone to see the external features of the devotee. It is also an offense to consider the empowered Vaiṣṇava an object of disciplinary action. It is offensive to give him advice or try to correct him."

In the *Caitanya-caritāmṛta* purports (*Madhya* 1.218-220 / *Madhya* 11.103) you have added:

"When a Vaiṣṇava sees that another Vaiṣṇava is a recipient of the Lord's mercy, he becomes very happy. Vaiṣṇavas are not envious. One who is envious of the success of a Vaiṣṇava is certainly not a Vaiṣṇava himself, but an ordinary mundane man and should not be respected but rejected. There are many jealous people in the dress of Vaiṣṇavas in the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement, and they should be completely neglected. Unfortunately, in this age of Kali, there are many mundane persons in the dress of Vaiṣṇavas, and Śrīla Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura has described them as disciples of Kali. He said, *'kali-cela.*' He indicated that there is another Vaiṣṇava, a pseudo-Vaiṣṇava, with *tilaka* on his nose and *kaṇṭhi* beads around his neck. A Vaiṣṇava *ācārya* is self-effulgent, and there is no need for any court judgment. A false *ācārya* may try to override a Vaiṣṇava by a high-court decision, but Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura says that he is nothing but a disciple of Kali-yuga."

"One must be considered bereft of the mercy of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu when he criticizes the Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement now spreading all over the world or finds fault with this movement or the leader of this movement."

The Lord's most recent appearance in the world was five-hundred years ago when He instructed us by playing the role of the ideal devotee, Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu. When the most powerful impersonalist of the time, Prakāśānanda Sarasvatī, heard about Lord Caitanya's chanting and dancing, he displayed his envy by telling his numerous followers that the Lord was only a sentimentalist and a magician. Prakāśānanda further instructed them not to listen to the Lord, thus, in the name of knowledge, doing the greatest disservice by misleading others from the path of the Absolute Truth. Impersonalists are always envious. Prakāśānanda would preach that the Supreme does not have a mouth, legs, or eyes, indirectly saying that God is dumb, lame, and blind. Because of such preaching, Prakāśānanda Sarasvatī was attacked with leprosy.

You are the spiritual master of the universe, yet we have seen those who have become careless with this razor's edge approach you with sliced and bleeding jugular veins. They are not limited to one race or locale. In India, those who willfully disobeyed the orders of your spiritual master, Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī, are the most notorious. Although their foul action, or lack of action, force your divine *guru* to depart early from this world, they have no humility. They envy you because you have followed. Such men are cursed and should be avoided like crocodiles in a swamp.

It is a known fact that, due to tight customs and quarantine surveillance, there are no snakes or tigers present in the lush tropical environment of Hawaii. But Bhaktivinoda Ṭhākura has said that a man who cannot be controlled by the higher instruction of Kṛṣṇa consciousness cannot me tamed. There is no such quarantine here for such a man. So many of them are sleeping in the darkness of this age. By delivering your message properly, they might be able to turn toward and become delivered. But for those who are pretending to sleep, whose minds wear the cloak of envy and the turban of greed, there is no possibility of becoming awakened. This we have seen.

You have told us that the material world is likened to a beehive. All the bees are scurrying about for the available honey, but, in the process, they are suffering the bites of one another. It is impossible to envy Lord Kṛṣṇa in His realm because He doesn't allow it. One is immediately shipped down and packed up in the immense material straightjacket. Therefore their business becomes envying and destroying one another.

You have shown us clearly that this material world is a battle. Prabhupāda, please allow us to accept you fully as the captain of our ship. Sometimes Kali, the evil symbol of sin, or his servant, throws a wave which tumbles and bewilders us atop this invaluable boat. Without your merciful guidance, we would have been long lost in that horrible ocean. Instead we are seeing, slowly but surely, that all good qualities are coming now as long as we abide by your order in this war against the illusory energy. You are requesting this of us. We are thus becoming more enthusiastic and confident. We thus are shining more brightly daily. As long as we remain serious and sincere, as long as we do not allow envy to reenter our hearts, the pock marks of the moon do not detract from it's splendor.

You are in pure and ecstatic love of Kṛṣṇa. Hawaii, Chicago, Geneva or Tokyo, it does not matter. Everywhere for you is Vṛndāvana, and you create the Vṛndāvana atmosphere for everyone anywhere you go. We, the devotees of ISKCON—Honolulu, pray that you allow us to ply this boat properly to it's destination and manifest our true moonlike nature. After all, it is your desire, and our only business is to eternally fulfill your desires, although our debt to you can never actually be repaid.

*Your servants at ISKCON—Honolulu*

## Houston

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate-bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śunyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

We prostrate ourselves before Your Divine Grace, begging your mercy this time and times forever more. You are the savior of the fallen—without you there is no one. No one to give us direction on the progressive march back to home, back to Godhead. Filled with lust, anger and greed, we couldn't understand the purpose of human life. But your kindness knows no bounds. Patiently and even with inconvenience to your divine self you have taught our fallen selves how to redirect these three gates to hell in such a perfect way that now our lust is turning into love, our anger into joy, and our greed into detachment. The darkness which was all-pervasive has been removed by your effulgent character, and we now all stand in the sun of Kṛṣṇa. Merely by the touch of the waves of the sacred Ganges one becomes liberated, even if he is the lowest of mankind; but by just seeing you, the same effect is also achieved. We also understand that to see you is to recall your instructions and act accordingly. Therefore we are all calling to you, "Give us the shade of your lotus feet and purify us!" Allow us to always follow your perfect instructions so that we may always please Your Divine Grace. This is our only wish, simply to do as you command. The puppet has no movement separate from the puppeteer. Similarly we will not move independently of your desires. Please give us the shelter of your lotus feet and direct us as you like.

*Your eternal servants,*

## Hyderabad

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Like the eternal causal ocean, your mercy for the fallen souls knows no limits. You have plucked us out of this ditch of materialistic life, which is compared (Cc. *Antya* 6.193) to a hole into which people pass stool, and now there is no way we can repay you but to eternally become your menial servants. How unfortunate that we are unable to understand all that you have done for us, for our seeing is of no value unless our eyes are fixed on you.

Our eyes were tightly closed shut and you forced them open with the transcendental secrets of your divine love. Until we met you, we had no knowledge of what real love was. Seeing your humility so sublime, a personality so true and kind, we've become so attracted that we are unable to leave your association, remaining your servants till the end of eternal time.

Please accept our sincere obeisances at your lotus feet, Śrīla Prabhupāda, even though we're so terribly unqualified, our hearts being full of sinful desires. All we can do is to try our hardest to serve you with determination and respect, hoping against hope, by Kṛṣṇa's mercy, we will develop loving devotional service to you, without any self-motivation. Force us by the power of your pure love to serve Lord Caitanya and accept this distribution of love of God as our very life and soul.

O master, give us the courage and strength to preach this sublime message throughout the world. *Māyā* is so strong that all we can do is constantly pray to you for protection. Please give us the intelligence to understand and accept your divine instructions, for you have become our intelligence to lead us back home, back to Godhead. May Kṛṣṇa give us strength, so that once having caught hold of your lotus feet, nothing will make us let go.

How fortunate for us that we've chosen to accept your mercy. You are so wealthy that you make no distinctions as to whom you give your mercy. You freely distribute the mercy of your books to everyone. Truly, how can we estimate the potency of these priceless gems—each one with the power to annihilate unlimited sinful activities. If it were not for you and your books, the world would have been devoid of knowledge of Kṛṣṇa. You have so kindly dedicated your life to translating the wealth of *bhakti* into so many languages, destroying the impersonalists, voidists and nonsense rascals that claim to be God. You have written so expertly that you shake the dull heads of the gross materialists and clearly establish the supremacy of Vaiṣṇavism. You are working so hard for us, Prabhupāda, that we think that you are trying to take the whole universe back to Kṛṣṇa with you. Surely, after reading all your books, who will not be attracted? For thousands of eons we will be able to taste the nectar of your association, through the medium of your books.

O master, take these worthless specks and place us along with the dust of your lotus feet. Now that we've tasted the nectar of your association we can think of nothing else worth striving for but to see your brilliant smile upon your insignificant servants. No words can describe the feeling we get when we see you smile; our hearts become filled with transcendental bliss. Surely Kṛṣṇa must have taught you all these wonderful things.

Of course you know, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that we may write many words about how much love we feel for you, but only by our activities can we actually prove that love. When we are able to give up everything, as you had, for preaching the glories of devotional service to Kṛṣṇa, without deviation, then we will really be able to speak of what love really is.

So now, following in the footsteps of our father, we will continue to preach your holy message for the benefit of all living entities. Lord Caitanya says:

"Whomever you meet, instruct him in the teachings of Kṛṣṇa. In this way, on My order, become a spiritual maser and deliver the people of this country." (Cc. *Madhya* 7.128)

Śrīla Prabhupāda and Lord Caitanya (Kṛṣṇa) have ordered us to deliver this message to everyone we meet, and so now our duty is clear—that we must accept these orders as our very life and soul and preach, preach, preach. Jaya Prabhupāda! May your transcendental love constantly increase.

*Your eternal servants:*

## Johannesburg and Durban

*yasya prasādād bhagavata-prasādo*  
*yasyāprasādān na gatiḥ kuto 'pi*  
*dhyāyaṁ stuvaṁs tasya yaśas tri-sandhyaṁ*  
*vande guroḥ śrī-caraṇāravindam*

By the mercy of the spiritual master, one receives the benediction of Kṛṣṇa. Without the grace of the spiritual master, one cannot make any advancement. Therefore, I should always remember my spiritual master. And at least three times a day I should offer my respectful obeisances unto the lotus feet of my spiritual master.

This verse, which has been given to us by Śrīla Viśvanātha Cakravartī Ṭhākura, carries with it the key of success in devotional service. As success in devotional service is due to the mercy of the spiritual master. Without the spiritual master one cannot actually know anything regarding devotional service, and without knowing anything about devotional service it is not possible to find any happiness in life, whether individually or collectively as a society.

Therefore, great saints and sages like Śrīla Vyāsadeva, Nārada Muni, and Prahlāda Mahārāja have concluded after careful study and consideration that the problems of life, especially those confronting our modern civilization, can only be solved by the method of widespread propaganda to promote the cult of *bhakti-yoga,* devotional service to the Supreme Personality of Godhead, Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa

The sufferings of the conditioned souls are due only to their being neglectful of the order of the Supreme Lord. And thus the conditioned souls are being forced by the laws of nature to transmigrate through the bodies of 8,400,000 species of life, where they must suffer the pains of *janma* (birth), *mṛtyu* (death), *jarā* (old age), and *vyādhi* (disease). Only when one comes in contact with the bona fide spiritual master is it possible to receive the seed of devotional service.

*brahmāṇḍa bhramite kona bhāgyavān jīva*  
*guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā bīja*

(According to their *karma,* all living entities are wandering throughout the entire universe. Some of them are being elevated to the upper planetary systems, and some of them are going down into the lower planetary systems. Out of many millions of living entities, one who is very fortunate gets an opportunity to associate with a bona fide spiritual master by the grace of Kṛṣṇa By the mercy of both Kṛṣṇa and the spiritual master, such a person receives the seed of the creeper of devotional service.)

After receiving the seed of devotional service from the bona fide *guru,* one has to then cultivate the creeper by the watering process, which is to chant the *mahā-mantra:* Hare Kṛṣṇa Hare Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa Kṛṣṇa Hare Hare/ Hare Rāma, Hare Rāma, Rāma Rāma Hare Hare. As the main creeper of devotional service begins to grow one has to take special caution to check any weeds with may try to kill the growth of the creeper. These weeds appear in the form of the ten offenses to the holy name.

While the watering process is going on one must also inquire from the spiritual master on matters regarding devotional service and one must render all kinds of service to him also. This will then provide good ground so that the creeper may spread its roots. All that then remains for the successful growth of the creeper is that it be placed under the open sky to receive the mercy of the suns rays. Thus it is by your mercy only, Śrīla Prabhupāda, that we are able to advance in Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You appear to us to be just like the shining sun.

It is our constant anxiety that when will the people of the world give up their foolish attachments to the cycle of birth and death and simply hear your messages of Godhead, as we have done, and thus become joyful.

How can we express with words our gratitude for your having bestowed upon us the ocean of Kṛṣṇa mercy? Our humble Vyāsa-Pūjā homage is like to offering of a small flame of fire to the sun globe. For what can we say to glorify you, which you have not taught us in the first place? Whatever we have heard from you we take as our heart and soul. We have no desire to hear from any other source however perfect it may appear to be, just like a chaste lady does not look to any other man other than her husband. We as your disciples do not know any other shelter than your lotus feet, which you have so kindly placed upon our unworthy heads in the form of your books. Therefore we hold your books close to our heart and we know that only by their distribution will the unfortunate inhabitants of this Kali-yuga be delivered.

Please accept whatever we have been able to say to show our love for you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, for we are like weak children before the powerful father.

*All Glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda!*

Africa's Johannesburg and Durban Yātrās

## Kṛṣṇa-Balarāma TSKP

**Meeting With Our Guru**

You call, "Where is Gurudāsa!" I come, expectant, eager to serve, prepared for the unexpected.

*Nama oṁ viṣṇu pādāya:* I offer my obeisances to the lotus feet of you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. *Kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhūtale:* you are very dear to Lord Kṛṣṇa.

Reverently I bow down, an air of eternity pervades the room, a seriousness of purpose found nowhere else. Conviction sets the mood and inspires me also, offering some small service, it becomes secondary, to the adventure for our mutual Lord, a small bluish boy. He can do so much. I try to write this but cannot express your greatness nor my love for you.

Your one knee is up in the air, like a great building of the future, your hand rests on the peak of your knee, your head rests back, relaxed, but you see everything.

When coming to see you a gravity is always there, even in our light joking which occurs often, interspersed with the business at hand. I watch interview after interview, with others also, sometimes in Hindi or Bengali, although the words are a montage passing by, the words still convey the same dedicated, serious love of Lord Kṛṣṇa—*dhira*—engaging whomever you meet, changing their thinking, towards a higher philosophical understanding, or to render some service, or if nothing else to accept some *prasāda.* "Give them *prasāda,*" you say as people are going. Now after watching you for so long I automatically do this, without asking.

People come to you, never taking your *darśana* lightly. You want to give them the highest goal of life, Kṛṣṇa consciousness. You want everyone to be happy, sometimes you have to administer the medicine by coaxing the seemingly bitter pill of surrender into them, sometimes cutting out the diseases we still carry, sometimes you engage us, as if we are holding a horse's mouth open and forcing the medicine in. Then with an encouraging word, whoever receives your mercy runs out of your room eager to execute the service you prescribe.

Anything may happen in your presence—you are transcendentally situated. You may question a new idea very carefully—"What makes you think your contribution is different?"—or you may ask "What is our goal? "To love Kṛṣṇa," we answer. "Why?" you challenge. Or you may ask, "How do you know Lord Kṛṣṇa is God?" You propose the answer and if it is not exact enough—"That is your dogmatism." You want us to be sure of what we are doing, many times you seem to question just to see if we are convinced or determined; if challenged and we remain silent "silence means affirmation" affirmation of whatever the opposing viewpoint is. Another time you questioned like a tornado, question after question. Each question was harder to answer. The questions took on technical rather than philosophical qualities, no bluffings, no abstractions. Śrīla Prabhupāda's voice thundering like a cyclone and tornado combined. I simply said, "I am a great fool"; you said extremely quiet and soft, "One must remain a fool before his spiritual master. Lord Caitanya said that." Then the mood became philosophical. By your questioning you train us to be careful, hard as iron, soft as a rose.

Sitting in your new Mercedes 200SL Lotus Chariot, our elbows sometimes touching, you speak to a life member in the front seat. You are so quiet, contained, sure, peaceful, powerful. All of Lord Kṛṣṇa comes from you; you are not separate from Lord Kṛṣṇa. Lord Kṛṣṇa stays in His abode, yet runs faster than everyone. You also stay and we cannot keep up with you, and when you travel we cannot keep up with you. You told of a Bengali proverb: an intelligent man can make thirteen rupees by sitting, another less intelligent man gets twelve rupees by traveling. Either way whether you travel or sit down we cannot keep up with you. Sometimes you sit so still your automatic watch stops.

Assorted facts fill the air, you are a storehouse of transcendental wisdom:

"Hot *jalebis* cure colds.

"Some rich men used to bury a live child with their hidden treasure so that when the child dies, he would become a ghost and guard the treasure.

"Mustard oil on the body, not in the body.

"Now as we import dry milk from the West when before there was an abundance of cows, now we are importing *brāhmaṇas* from the West.

"A language does not consist of yes, no, very good.

"Do things cooperatively, for if you fail, no one person is to blame and if there is success, all persons get credit.

"The scientists research useless things. They say the poison is colorless, odorless and tasteless. How can they test that the poison is tasteless?" (And you imitate a dying scientist whose last words are "It is tasteless!")

"There were *yogīs* who were able to eat mercury and then make gold from their urine." Then you laugh, "That is how they maintain themselves."

"They say that you don't see Kṛṣṇa. How they can say you don't see, if they don't see? Just like you may have a lover and when you think of that lover you see, but the other person doesn't see your lover. They cannot see that you do not see Kṛṣṇa. A man in Bengal was a writer and there was an accident in front of his window. He did not see because he was absorbed in writing. We are absorbed in Kṛṣṇa, we can see Him." Little anecdotes spice your teachings.

As the Bengali countryside passes I think of the years you have sacrificed for us all, leaving the wonderful shelter of Rādhā-Dāmodara to execute your Guru's orders. We too must go where you bid us, do what pleases us most.

Floating down the roads of Bengal, we ride silently in your new 200SL chariot, you view everything with clear blazing vision, curiosity with spiritual perspective combined. I too, like a child, try to view everything that passes by the window, so much going on, so much to see, everything living, everything personal, children, bullock carts, straw huts, bathing *ghats,* water buffalo, dogs, bicycles, *dahl* drying on the road, palm trees. So much to see, a variety of senses, yet I am pulled to see you, to disregard all that is going on around and gaze on your lotus face as the Bengal scenery floats by, as if the planet's zooming instead of the trees. That is your divine trick, I see *you* instead of the externals. You sit with your hand in the air, a flower between your long golden fingers, caressed by you, remaining ever fresh, unharmed during the whole journey. Your face concerned for the whole of humanity, your eyes beam compassionately, eyes no one can equal, combined with an unshakable strength, you see everything, your feet planted firmly on the ground, relaxed, sure of Lord Kṛṣṇa's protection, you inspire the devotees of the world.

I see you instead of the variegatedness, you have attracted me to the divine instead of the mundane, you constantly trick everyone this way, "Think of Lord Kṛṣṇa, not *māyā,*" and because every movement of your eyes is preaching, you constantly engage in Kṛṣṇa's service anyone who meets you.

You scoff at the material world, the same one that everyone embraces, yet you remain a gentleman in its domain. You tell humorous story upon story to describe the absurdity of fallible soldiers of illusion and through humor we can laugh at ourselves, at humanity's useless struggle and then try to embrace a higher taste. You tell us that material life means falldown, still the foolish man embraces the temporary pleasures and pains despite his seeing everything decay before him. Despite his brothers being jailed, he steals. You mock the foolishness. A man is sawing a branch (you demonstrate), sawing himself on the limb, cutting in such a way that he will fall. His friend comes and says, "If you cut that way you will fall down." "Don't tell me how to cut, I know," says the blinded fool, and when he falls on the ground he says to his friend, "Oh, you are a great astrologer, you can see the future."

Another story teaches us to be responsible for our actions. A man is beating someone with a trick, the police constable comes and charges you are beating that man. "Oh, no, it is not me beating him, it is the stick." You demonstrate that we must be responsible and not blame others.

We blunder with the blunt tool of narrow minds, not seeing that you want us to understand Kṛṣṇa in a living spontaneous manner. Instead we try to make His teachings a geometric cement and steel formula. You teach us to be real devotees, not artificial but joyous and loving, sages who honor time and circumstance and we still choose to embrace hard and fast rules.

I remember years ago how you told us of the *smarta brāhmaṇa* who had two fish-shaped brass little dishes, *koṣit* and *koṣu.* This *brāhmaṇa* was pouring oblations of Ganges water back into the river. After finishing the oblations he put his two brass fish-shaped cups on the river bank and went to say his Gāyatrī *mantra.* After Gāyatrī, upon returning to the bank to get his cups, he saw cups of the same size and shape. The other *brāhmaṇas* had left them on the bank. The next day, to avoid the same mix-up, he placed a small clay ball in the cups, before saying his Gāyatrī. When he went to retrieve his cups he saw all the *brāhmaṇas'* cups had little clay balls in them, as the thought that the clay ball was an esoteric ritual that they did not know, so being *smarta brāhmaṇas* they imitated blindly. "Sarble sing is dead," and half-hen logic were given at that time also.

How can we count the gifts you give us, you are training us all, lions, tigers, dogs, cats, gangsters and cheats, like a lion tamer in the circus of illusions. Mad men prisoners in the material world, you point out "who is crazy and who is sane." For ten years I meditate on how you try to show us that we are not this body. We still take refuge in this cage of mucus, bile, stool and bone. If we realized this simple fact there would not be any fighting. Again you illustrate with example and story. "When someone calls you *prabhu,* you should not think 'Come here and wipe my shoes.'" We are like children beating their father while arguing over which part to massage. The tendency to be envious is recounted by this dialogue: "did you hear of such and such a high court judge?" "No, it cannot be, he is not qualified to be a high court judge." "No, no it is true, he is a high court judge." "Alright, he may be a high court judge, but he must not be getting any salary!"

You warn us not to cheat, and I realize that you know that we are not very advanced. It is not a cheap thing. We vacillate from devotee to cheat a thousand times daily. Again you have to spoon-feed us with stories, as the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam* and *Bhagavad-gītā* are not imbibed in our frivolous state. This you illustrate with the story of a motivated devotee, the fictitious man from Bengal, Gopāla Bon, who is stringing a garland of *bokul* flowers. (*Bokul* is a flower as well as a vegetable mixed with other vegetables.) His friend asks, "What are you doing?" "Oh, I am making a garland for Lord Kṛṣṇa," says Gopāla. "do you mean to say that Lord Kṛṣṇa will come here and take this garland from you?" his friend asked. "That is my plan. If Lord Kṛṣṇa doesn't come and take this garland, then I shall fry it up and eat it." Another time Gopāla Bon is walking down the road with a basket of wheat on his head, and a strong wind comes and scatters the wheat in all ten directions. Then Gopāla folds his hands together and says, "Govinda bhoga."

Riding in the car, back seat, sitting next to you, the "Kṛṣṇa Meditation" tape penetrating, the joyous sound of your unmatchable harmonium filling our hearts with glee, you turn and say, "Gurudāsa Prabhu, can you play the harmonium like that?" and I realize that you are the best at everything. So many muscular youths fall behind in your transcendental wake. I feel proud sitting with you in the car, not only because it is a special privilege, but because I am proud of you and of being your son and disciple. You outclass us in all respects, as you have the qualities of Kṛṣṇa. Therefore you are the *guru.* You can walk farther than any of us in the mornings, dance nicer, think quicker, quote more *ślokas,* open more temples, preach more miles, write more essays, poems, books, and letters, eat less *prasāda,* eat more *prasāda* if the mood strikes, sleep less, read more, think of Kṛṣṇa more, sing the most beautifully, distribute the most books, attract the most members, make friends with the most devotees, remain transcendentally aloof, the most detached, the most dependent on Kṛṣṇa, a traveling mendicant and simultaneously the most wealthy, able to smile broader than all, and then one second later appear the gravest. Softness caresses the corner of your eyes, fire blazes from the center, intelligence from the middle, you are able to hold a flower longer than all of us without crushing it, you know us all, we do not know you, and you allow familiarity. You become childlike and then suddenly if we misuse this, you cut us down like a banana in a whirlwind, you disappear as Lord Kṛṣṇa did when the *gopīs* became proud. You encourage us again and again, using a story as you coax us to be bold for Kṛṣṇa.

We ask can we put on dramatic plays in the street, you say, "Why not, do it with Nṛsiṁha faces, what you do all will follow. It will become a style." Then you again tell of a Charles Chaplain film you say fifty years before. As follows: Charles Chaplain was at the ball dance (fancy dress ball) and when he went to sit some rascal nailed down his tailing coat. When Charles Chaplain got up, his tailing coat tore in the middle, he did not know it, and when he danced everyone was looking at his torn coat. Charles Chaplain went into the private room and saw in the mirrors that his tails were torn up the middle and so he tore the tail all the way up to the neck and he danced harder as they flapped around. All the people saw this and they too went into the private room and tore their tailing coats and danced. It became the style." Later: "Be bold, if you're going to hunt try for the rhinoceros, and if you're going to thieve go for the government reserve. If you fail, everyone will say "It was impossible," and if you succeed, everyone will say "How great."

You taught us again as we rode to Ṭhākura Bhaktivinoda's Dvādaśī Mandir in Bir Nagar, West Bengal. The horn of our old green car failed. Bhavānanda Svāmī and myself were yelling Hare Kṛṣṇa out the window at all the obstacles on the road, so as to try and warn them of the car coming, but they sometimes did not hear. You then said, "Give me my plate," and you took your cane and banged on the plate and said, "This is our horn." It worked. We banged all the way to Bir Nagar and you laughed. "They will think that this is the latest American style horn and will want to imitate," and you laughed again.

In your history of India lessons to me you made the same point, that anything "made in Britain" was considered first-class. You told how the English came to India and instilled the whole population with the idea of buying British cloth, and how the British administrators were paid in gold via "the home bill," thus depleting the gold reserve, making India dependent on England. Because of this dependency India felt sheltered by Britain and so anything "made in England," done in England, was the style. It still exists today, you go on, "The villager asks if I vote for the British, will they care?" All these stories would not be necessary if we were able to hear you correctly. The *śāstra* simply states the same thing:

*yad yad ācarati śreṣṭhas*  
*tat tad evetaro janaḥ*  
*sa yat pramāṇam kurute*  
*lokas tad anuvartate*  
*(Bg. 3.21)*

**"Whatever action is performed by a great man, common men follow in his footsteps. And whatever standards he sets by exemplary acts, all the world pursues."**

But we need spoon feeding and you take your precious time to do this like a caring mother dropping nectar in our yearning beaks. So we follow in your footsteps and follow your exemplary acts. Now let the whole world pursue.

Your eyes moist, everyone awaiting your next move, you grace us all individually, all different personalities, yet one in your presence. One frivolous becomes serious. The dry become juicy. We must not forget you, we must remain *dhīra* always. We must understand time and circumstances and mostly Prabhus, we must not fight anymore, we must read your instructions, your books daily and chant in-between. You have given us the mercy. Recently on a walk I asked "How does *kṛpā-siddhi* work?" You turned on me suddenly and said, "If I give you a hundred thousand rupees and say, 'Take it, take it,' that is *kṛpā-siddhi,*" and then to emphasize you put your hand in my stomach giving me the imaginary 100,000 rs., saying "Take it, take it! And when you spend, you realize—*kṛpā-siddhi.*"

It is you who are giving us Kṛṣṇa. We must take His lotus feet on our head. This is our goal, nothing else. You and He are not separate, that we must realize. I love you, you make me happy. *Guru-kṛṣṇa-prasāde pāya bhakti-latā-bīja.* "Now go on and help me push on this movement." We leave your chambers, a new determination. Kṛṣṇa please engage me in your service. Hare Kṛṣṇa.

*Your servant,*  
Gurudāsa Svāmī  
Kṛṣṇa-Balarama Traveling Saṅkīrtana Party

## Laguna Beach

We hope we may invoke the pleasure  
of he whose glories have no measure,  
the servant of the lotus feet of God,  
The bringer of the mahā-mantra,  
His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta  
Swami Mahārāja Prabhupāda.  
He heard from the authority,  
Śrīla Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī  
Who entrusted him with Lord Caitanya's teachings,  
Then acting at the Lord's behest,  
He sailed to the degraded West  
to satisfy his master by his preaching.  
He found is in the degradation  
of illicit sex, intoxication,  
gambling and unrestricted killing.  
But by his mercy and his kindness,  
he cured us of your sinful blindness  
and picked us up when no one else was willing.  
We were surely on our way to hell,  
thinking our bodies to be ourselves  
and thinking God impersonal of nothing.  
But he showed us that we're spirit souls,  
servants of the Spirit whole—  
Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa personal and loving.  
He saves us from mundane frustration  
and spiritual self-annihilation,  
appearing as our transcendental savior  
To bring us back to bhāgavata-dharma,  
Unalloyed by jñāna and karma,  
engaging us in serving Kṛṣṇa favor.  
He boldly challenged scientists,  
religionists and atheists  
and members of the Māyāvāda school.  
Showing us that one who's wise  
is never one to compromise,  
he plainly called them rascals, rogues and fools.  
Many men had come before,  
promising to show us more  
but the came just to bluff us and to cheat.  
yet he came not to take our money,  
but to let us taste the honey  
flowing from Lord Kṛṣṇa lotus feet.  
We cannot hope to fully glorify or understand  
The confidential servant from the transcendental land  
who saved us from our miserable condition.  
O Prabhupāda! We therefore beg for but a drop of your compassion  
So that we may always be engaged in transcendental action  
to further spread Your Lordship's holy mission.

Dhumrakeśa dāsa adhikārī / Dṛḍha-vrata dāsa adhikārī / Yuga-dharma dāsa adhikārī / Gupta dāsa adhikārī / Ṛṣabhadeva dāsa adhikārī / Ratnanābha dāsa adhikārī / Siṁheśvara dāsa brahmacārī / Bhojadeva dāsa brahmacārī / Bhakta Mark / Bhakta Chris / Bhakta Leon / Bhakta Wayne / Bhakta Lenny / Cinmayī-devī dāsī / Śrīśa-devī dāsī / Nayika-devī dāsī / Pāpa-hariṇī-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Beverly

## London (Bhaktivedanta Manor)

*oṁ ajñāna-timirāndhasya jñānāñjana-śalākayā*  
*cakṣur unmīlitaṁ yena tasmai śrī-gurave namaḥ*

You have come, a great sun, to brighten our day  
To deliver from evil all those long astray  
The condemned, the fallen, so useless a host  
All babbling, all crazy, all living as ghosts  
Captive by māyā's soft glittering hands  
Conditioned we wandered in these wretched lands  
In this rotten pool of saṁsāra we've played  
You have come, a great sun, to brighten our day.  
Shining, the lamp of knowledge you hold  
Standing alone in a dark land so bold  
You cut through our darkness, uproot our decay  
You have come, a great sun, to brighten our day  
So patient, you tell of the Lord's personal form  
He appears to take birth, though He's never born  
Absolute is sentient thou also proclaim  
You have come, a great sun, to brighten our day  
You have given us everything, O Prabhupāda, it's true  
There's nothing that cannot be known from you  
O Jagad-guru, all our fears you allay  
You have come, a great sun, to brighten our day  
Of us you ask little, just to chant Kṛṣṇa's name  
To preach His glories and spread His fame  
To always stay pure and happy and sane,  
You laugh like the sun, we're real people again!

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

I beg that you will somehow or other accept our utterly insignificant *daṇḍavats* in the dust of your lotus feet.

But for you, we would be eternally condemned to the hopeless labyrinth of birth and death. But for you, our every action would fasten ever tighter the iron shackles of illusion. But for you, we could never taste the mercy of Their Lordships Balarāma, Subhadrā, Jagannātha. And but for you, we should sink ever more bewildered to the foulest depths of dark confusion.

But for you, actual happiness would be no more than a hollow promise, forever broken. But for you, the endless nectar if the holy name for millions would remain untasted. But for you, we could never know so many words of mercy, timely spoken. And but for you, for us and countless others, our human life would be long since wasted.

Mere words are worthless, even parrots can cite poetry in meter and in rhyme. Likewise our words are empty too, though they may glitter like the sunshine on the marble white of dead mens' tombs. But by your mercy you can take these scanty, colored sheets and lifeless lines, and with your sharp perception maybe find a shaft of light that hides, perchance, within the gloom.

For you dear lord, are like unto the noblest bird, the swan, who sifts the priceless milk from puddles full of water-weeds and rain.

And thus you taste the endless sea of blissful nectar which come second unto none, by ever meditating on the lotus feet of Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa and spreading far the glories of Their holy name.

*Your worthless servants,* London Bhaktivedanta Manor

## London (Bury Place)

Long ago the Lord did impart  
transcendental knowledge into Lord Brahmā's heart  
The supreme spiritual science he did unfold  
to help the souls lost in the material world  
This material body, a decaying shell  
in which the spirit soul does dwell  
Servants of Kṛṣṇa we all are  
eternally existing brighter than a star  
Kṛṣṇa the Supreme Lord of all creation  
the original person, the source of all emanations  
The supreme personality, glorious beyond all  
yet he sports before us as a little cowherd boy  
He gives a chance for souls gone astray  
to taste spiritual bliss and find the true way  
This perfect knowledge was then handed down  
by very great souls in a disciplic chain  
From Brahmā to Nārada and then to Vyāsa  
in this way millions of years did pass  
From sage to sage the message traveled  
in age after age its glory was unraveled  
These great saintly personalities, their prowess unequalled  
dreamed of the day when all of the people  
sunk in the mire of material existence  
would be able to hear this great spiritual science  
Then Lord Caitanya, the crest of all incarnations,  
appeared in the line of disciplic succession  
He cultivated the tree of pure love of God  
and freely distributed the fruits grown thereon  
As with the sages his desire was the same  
that every town and village would glorify God's name  
London to Los Angeles, let everywhere resound  
With the purifying vibration of transcendental sound  
Never forgetting what you had been told  
this order became your life and soul  
Years passed waiting for the time to appear  
to execute the mission that you held so dear  
Then late in life, dressed in saffron robe  
you began the work that would change the globe  
there in a small room in the holy dhām  
you began to translate Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam  
the spotless Purāṇa, brilliant as the sun  
the world's greatest scripture, second to none  
to mitigate the miseries of all classes of men  
that very ancient science was now spoken again  
Oldest of all but in a new dress  
miracle done, Your Divine Grace  
thus forsaking the Lord's Vṛndāvana home  
into Kali's domain you ventured alone  
with nothing but faith in God you set sail  
and alighted on the shores of Kali's capital  
Without mercy, friends or any material tender  
but with something much greater—complete self-surrender  
You came and returned to us what we had lost  
the most sublime treasure, pure love of God  
Armed with the Bhāgavatam's perfect knowledge  
with undaunting spirit you began to preach  
The rest of the story will be indelibly inscribed  
into the annals of history for all recorded time  
The historians of eternity will tell and retell  
of your great mercy errand to deliver souls from hell  
He injected this desire into the disciplic line  
and hence it was carried until modern times  
Your Guru Mahārāja gave you the same instruction  
"Take these teachings to the English-speaking nations"  
and the pious people of some future generation  
will listen with wonder and great veneration  
Wherever the stories of the great saints are received  
there will be descriptions of the miracles you achieved  
We are a testimony to this miracle, Gurudeva  
each one of us from abominable life, you did save  
Your gentle hand guiding us away from confusion  
away from the evils of desire, hate and delusion  
lifting us from his hell on earth  
giving us knowledge, a second birth  
taking us out of māyā's filthy pit  
giving us devotional service though we be unfit  
teaching us to remember Kṛṣṇa, the Supreme Lord  
and giving us a taste of the spiritual world  
Prabhupāda everything we owe to you  
you have made our lives so fresh and new  
Where there was darkness now there is light  
Where there was blindness now there is sight  
Where there was dreaming in dark dingy rooms  
now there is nectar and places of touchstone  
Where there was cooking and eating only sin  
now there is Kṛṣṇa's delicious prasāda  
Where there was hard toil with scant reward  
now we render service unto the Supreme Lord  
Where there was envy and intense competition  
now there is harmony and holy association  
Where there was loneliness in the world of praise and blame  
now there is communal chanting of the Lord's holy name  
We are eternally indebted for the sacrifice you have made  
so foolish to think that you can ever be repaid  
Whatever we have to offer is so insignificant and small  
but still you accept it, because you're the most merciful of all  
We are simply praying that no matter what difficulties we may meet  
we can always remain serving you lotus feet

*Thank you Śrīla Prabhupāda*

Your eternal servants at the London Temple

## Los Angeles

*My divine master,*

Your transcendental mind and attention are perpetually focused, in unalloyed devotion, upon the wonderful transcendental forms, names, qualities and pastimes of Their Lordships Śrī Śrī Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa in Braja. Your mind is completely transcendental and ecstatic, relishing the sublime mellows of devotional service at every moment. Yet, falling at your lotus feet in great humility, I urgently pray to you, my eternal master, to kindly turn your attention to my wretched self because, as fallen and unqualified as I am, I want to submit a prayer and an urgent appeal to you and beg you to kindly hear me:

**A Disciple's Prayer to the Lotus Feet of His Eternal, Well-Wishing Spiritual Master**

*O my lord,*

I am an eternal spirit soul, part and parcel of Kṛṣṇa. Somehow or other at an unknown time in the remote past, I became covered by Kṛṣṇa's illusory energy, *māyā,* and I thought that I could enjoy life independently of Him. I then fell from the spiritual world, which is my eternal home, to the material world, which is nothing but a horrible place of suffering. Since that time I have been forced, for countless scores of births, to inhabit one body after another, appearing sometimes as a demigod, sometimes as a dog and sometimes in a lower form. In this way, I suffered terribly. Helplessly lost in the whirlpool of material illusion, I was completely mad, forgetting my eternal nature and my real constitutional position. Ignorant of my spiritual nature, I ran after material sense enjoyment, but was repeatedly kicked and frustrated in my attempt to be happy in that way. In such a state of frightening, hellish madness, I performed many horrible and unmentionable acts, which brought myself and others great pain. At the same time, other similarly illusioned living entities performed equally horrible actions which wrought great pain on me. Performing cruel and sinful actions based on ignorance, and impelled by a false desire to enjoy matter, I was forced to suffer the consequences, perpetually. Thus there was no limit to my suffering. At times, in the painful sojourn of my material existence, I came to appreciate the futility of sense enjoyment in the material world and considered material enjoyment to be full of miseries. However, due to my strong bodily conception, my memory was destroyed, and again and again I ran after material enjoyment just as an animal runs after a mirage in the desert. Lifetime after lifetime after lifetime, this horrible suffering continued without any relief.

II

*My eternal preceptor,*

In this present lifetime, however, by the *inconceivable* causeless mercy of the Supreme Lord, you came to save me. As a great Mahā-bhāgavata Paramahaṁsa Vaiṣṇava, you are eternally attached to Kṛṣṇa and are therefore fully enlightened, blissful and self-satisfied. Yet, due to your *inconceivable* mercy, the thought of which melts my stone heart and makes me cry, you took pity on me.

First, you made me give up my strong addiction to grossly sinful habits such as flesh-eating, intoxication and illicit sex, which degrade the soul. Then, you taught me how to chant the Hare Kṛṣṇa *mahā-mantra*—the transcendental sound vibration which awakens the conditioned soul from his sleeping condition. You even showed me, a filthy animal, how to keep my body clean—I did not even know how to pass stool properly and you instructed me in the proper method. You put clean saffron cloth on my sinful body and taught me how to dance before the Deity. You prepared wonderful, pure foodstuffs, offered them in devotion to Kṛṣṇa and fed me the remains until I could eat no more.

As I gradually became a bit more civilized, you began to instruct me in the proper aim of human life. You revealed to me, through your commentaries on *Bhagavad-gītā* and *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam,* that I am not this temporary material body, but an infinitesimal spirit soul, constitutionally eternal (*sat*), full of knowledge (*cit*) and blissful (*ānanda*); that I am part and parcel of the *Supreme* Soul, Kṛṣṇa, who is an unlimitedly sweet cowherd boy; that I have an *eternal,* intimate, sublime relationship with Him based on transcendental loving service and surrender; that, due to impure desires, I fell from Kṛṣṇa's abode in the spiritual world and became tightly bound up in the clutches of Kṛṣṇa's illusory energy in a suffering condition, transmigrating from one material body to another; that this human form of life is a very great and rare gift of the material nature because, being equipped with higher and more subtle intelligence, it affords the conditioned soul a chance understand his existential position and to revive his original spiritual consciousness, or Kṛṣṇa consciousness, thus getting out of material bondage. Finally, you took time to instruct me in all the procedures of *sādhana-bhakti,* or the regulative purificatory stage of devotional service, whereby the spiritual aspirant gradually reawakens his dormant, blissful, transcendental consciousness. In this way, you firmly and compassionately lifted me from a condition of madness to a sane condition of relative peacefulness, happiness and knowledge, wherein the miseries of material existence have been reduced to almost nil.

III

*My merciful chastiser,*

However, not caring for your mercy, I have not fixed Kṛṣṇa consciousness in my heart, as my life and soul. Becoming complacent and puffed up, I have maintained many material attachments and impurities.

My enthusiasm for engaging in service is sparked by the desire to be glorified and honored by others and for material profit. If these rewards do not come, I become bewildered in my determination. I am full of lust and secretly think of mundane sex pleasure while outwardly pretending to be a detached *sādhu.* I run here and there to gratify the tongue, and if I cannot eat to the neck, I become morose. I am always speaking nonsense and becoming enlivened when others hear and appreciate my loose talk. Forgetting that the other devotees are your parts and parcels—that they are your eternal personal servants—I deal with them in an arrogant and impersonal way, sometimes speaking harshly and sometimes criticizing unnecessarily. When I see another disciple advancing in Kṛṣṇa consciousness, I become envious, and when a disciple falls down, I secretly feel satisfaction.

Because my mind is always disturbed by multifarious, personally-motivated desires, I cannot fix my mind on the process of advancement on Kṛṣṇa consciousness. And because my vision is clouded and narrowed by such selfish desires, I lack the quality of compassion and consequently am unable to share in your great vision and magnanimous plan to save the world. My ingratefulness knowing no limit, I try to forget you, because the remembrance of your holy self causes me to feel ashamed of my fallen condition, my selfish schemes and my lack of loyalty to your lotus feet. This is my horrible and lamentable condition. Thinking of all these personal faults and shortcomings, I cry in shame.

IV

*Therefore, my eternal divine master,*

In this lowly and unfortunate condition, I humbly pray to your merciful lotus feet to please take pity on this useless soul, who is so devoid of good qualities, noble intentions and pure aspirations. I am a fool! A fool! A weak and petty-minded fool! But you have planted in this fool's heart the seed of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. This valuable seed, however, is being burned up by the fire of material lust that burns in my heart. Please save the seed which you have planted there! How fallen am I and how great is Kṛṣṇa consciousness! This thought tears through my mind and heart like a hot iron rod. Please kick me hard with your lotus feet that I may wake up from this dangerous deep sleep of forgetfulness of the prime duty of human life. Clasping your lotus feet in my hands and pressing them on my chest, I humbly beg you to pick me up. Without Kṛṣṇa consciousness I am no better than an dead man. Now I want to give up all my nonsense. I want to see the light of Kṛṣṇa consciousness. I don't want to go on sleeping in ignorance. I surrender to you, Śrīla Prabhupāda. Please fix me eternally at your lotus feet and protect me.

*Your disciples in the New Dvārkā community*

## Madras

Let us offer our respectful obeisances unto His Divine Grace A.C. Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupāda, who out of his great compassion for every living entity suffering in material existence, has translated the essence of Vedic knowledge into the most palatable words that have entered our ears.

Śrīla Prabhupāda, you are verily a holy place personified, because you carry the Personality of Godhead within your heart; you turn all places into places of pilgrimage. Who can measure your glories, Śrīla Prabhupāda? You are doing what no one else has ever done. In South India, there are many large temples which have many *brāhmaṇas* reciting Vedic *mantras,* but none of their activities are attracting persons to become devotees of Śrī Caitanya Mahāprabhu and Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa. You are the Jagad-guru, and the actual *ācārya,*  as your activities prove. Please let us always hold tightly to your lotus feet, by being constantly immersed in your instructions. Thank you for picking us up, Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*Your eternal servants,*

Bhavabhūti dāsa brahmacārī / Śravanānanda dāsa brahmacārī / Prasannātmā dāsa brahmacārī / Tāraka dāsa brahmacārī / Vijaya dāsa brahmacāṛi / Narasiṁha Mūrti dāsa brahmacārī

## Mauritius

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate-bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śunyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dearest Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most humble obeisances. First of all I must regret that I am not qualified to properly give you the glorification that is called for on this most glorious occasion of Vyāsa-Pūjā. For glorifying you, the prolific talents in writing of great persons like Sūta Gosvāmī, Kṛṣṇa dāsa Kavirāja Gosvāmī. Vṛndāvana dāsa Ṭhākura and Rūpa Gosvāmī can alone give proper testimonial to the fact of your unlimited greatness. Only persons such as these can accurately describe your sublime characteristics. Our attempt to glorify you is only like that of the child whose learning process is always sloppy and crude. Therefore kindly bear the faulty expression and irregular composition which fill this prayer and accept only our ardent desire to be engaged as your servants.

Any gentleman will readily accept that you are Lord Kṛṣṇa's most confidential servant. This is confirmed in *Bhagavad-gītā* by the Lord's own words. "For one who explains this supreme secret to the devotees, devotional service is guaranteed, and at the end he will come to Me. There is no servant in this world more dear to Me than he, nor will there ever be one more dear." (Bg. 18.68-69) Therefore, because you are broadcasting the message of *Bhagavad-gītā* the most widely, purely, and effectively throughout the world, we can clearly understand from the Lord's own words that you are His most dear servant. There is no doubt about this, and therefore we are most anxious to please you.

You are the transcendental revolutionary. When you came to Mauritius and were greeted by so many public officials, you did not engage in "boot licking" as is the fashion among other so called *svāmīs.* Rather you immediately took the opportunity to criticize the faulty standards based in ignorance and in detail described to your captive audience how they could restructure their entire economic and social base to make the actual heaven on earth. Quite a surprise it was to those gentlemen who had expected only a sentimental lecture on "Hinduism" and in the turn of events received your transcendental boot in the face of misconception. You do not compromise truth in the face of material respect and gain. We have actually seen this.

You are the greatest transcendental general leading the troops of Caitanya Mahāprabhu. Whenever you maneuver your troops the strategic position if the enemy is broken if not lost. We are simply thunderstruck by your transcendental feats, which go on increasing and increasing in the most inconceivable way. On whatever town or village we march by your order, no matter where it may be, as the transcendental vibrations are fired by different weapons, everyone, whether envious or nonenvious, stands up to take notice. No one can actually be indifferent to your transcendental firing squad. Therefore, this Kṛṣṇa consciousness movement is loaded with transcendental happiness and you are the chief supplying agent giving us the unlimited ammunition of your wisdom and love. This is our experience.

The stride of a gentleman is firm, even bold, this voice holds the same gravity. Your heart is fixed, strong and determined to be full of love of Kṛṣṇa, and friendly to every living being. This we have actually seen.

Therefore, it has also become our ambition and determination to become gentlemen. We have no business with harsh words, envy, or jealousy. Such trash should not be scattered in the lovely parks where devotional service takes place, but should be burnt up in the fire of your instructions. In this way, feeling lowly and insignificant as a natural inclination, the position of the forebearing grass and the tolerant tree begins to make perfect sense by your grace, whereas to the untrained observer such a situation is the situation of a fool. This has actually been our experience.

Dear spiritual master, dear lord, I am being beaten into submission by the laws of material nature and I cannot but wait for the day when my head finally touches the ground in complete obedience under the protection of your lotus feet and being unable to answer to the call of *māyā,* I will smell the actual fragrance of lotus feet.

Kindly favor us, your servants, so that we may take part in your transcendental pastimes of saving the fallen souls. Life after life this is our aspiration.

*Your servants,*

Prabhanu dāsa brahmacārī / Śrī-niketana dāsa brahmacārī / Arjuna dāsa brahmacārī / Nitāi-cānda dāsa brahmacārī

## Māyāpur

Bhaktisiddhānta Sarasvatī Ṭhākura  
sent disciples over the sea  
But his Bhaktivedanta Swami Prabhupada  
Alone planted the devotional tree.  
Chanting Hare Kṛṣṇa, dancing, prasāda,  
Spreading the holy message of Gaura;  
Śrīla Prabhupāda teaches Gītā, Bhāgavata  
without mixing in anything more.  
Other so-called sādhus go abroad,  
Soon to come back to their land.  
A failure only earning cheap applause  
Without making any permanent stand.  
Jaya Prabhupāda, for helping others find the way,  
And weeding voidism and impersonalism from the West.  
Watering and caring for our bhakti tree every day,  
New life, happy day, filled ever with zest.  
Fallen from the path of divine knowledge  
Working hard like a dog, to enjoy like a hog,  
We offer thousands of obeisances unto you, O great sage!  
For purifying our vision from the fog.  
Holy name sung by ecstatic mṛdaṅga beat  
Goes well for spreading the holy name around.  
Prabhupāda's devotees with books on every street,  
Here's the Big mṛdaṅga carrying farthest the holy sound!  
Sarasvatī Ṭhākura Bhaktivinoda's footsteps mark he path,  
Śrīla Prabhupāda guides us blind men up that holy road.  
Develop Māyāpur, wonderful Lord Caitanya's place of birth.  
Living of dying is the same when living in the spiritual abode!  
Holy Bhārata-varṣa misdirected, lost her sight,  
By Prabhupāda's mercy may her culture be restored.  
Last in India ashen-covered embers scatter light  
Western bhaktas bring fresh to mind the Golden Lord.  
The materialist is wrong is your song,  
without Kṛṣṇa there is no way to be right.  
A sense-restricted scientist's song must be wrong,  
Your Divine Grace's arguments are watertight.  
ISKCON was created by Your Divine Grace  
To stand united against the darkness of nescience.  
We most fallen Māyāpur nivāsī's beg a humble place,  
Eternally to serve Your Lotus Feet with awakened senses.

## Melbourne

*nama oṁ viṣṇu-pādāya kṛṣṇa-preṣṭhāya bhū-tale*  
*śrīmate-bhaktivedānta-svāmin iti nāmine*

*namas te sārasvate deve gaura-vāṇī-pracāriṇe*  
*nirviśeṣa-śunyavādi-pāścātya-deśa-tāriṇe*

*Dear Śrīla Prabhupāda,*

Please accept our most fallen obeisances at your lotus feet. As stated in the *Śrīmad-Bhāgavatam*:

*kirāta-hūṇāndhra-pulinda-pulkaśā*  
*ābhīra-śumbhā yavanāḥ khasādayaḥ*  
*ye 'nye ca pāpā yad-apāśrayāśrayāḥ*  
*śudhyanti tasmai prabhaviṣṇave namaḥ*

**Kirāta, Hūṇa, Śumbha, Yavana and Khasa races and even others who are addicted to sinful acts can be purified by taking shelter of the devotees of the Lord due to His being the supreme power. I beg to offer my respectful obeisances unto Him." (*****SB*** **2.4.18)**

You are such a pure devotee, and even the most illusioned and sinful living entities cannot continue their degraded activities if they have the association of a pure devotee like you. By your mercy, you are giving everyone the chance of becoming Kṛṣṇa conscious, the greatest boon of this human form of life. By your mercy, a dumb man can speak, and a lame man can cross over a mountain; and so we who are so unqualified, on this auspicious day, can simply offer you our prostrated obeisances again and again, praying to take complete shelter of your lotus feet, because by taking such shelter one can be protected from the glamor of *māyā.*

We can only hope to be able to serve you birth after birth, life after life, unflinchingly, with firm faith and determination to chant Hare Kṛṣṇa and follow all the rules and regulations of *bhakti* life. Without you, Śrīla Prabhupāda, our lives are meaningless. You have opened up our eyes with the torchlight of transcendental knowledge, so therefore we pray, "Please engage us eternally in your service!"

All Glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda.

*The devotees of Melbourne Mandira*

## Mexico

Ladies and gentlemen, Śrīla Prabhupāda, the best among the Vaiṣṇavas and the protector of his devotees, is the most exalted personality in the whole universe (*jagad-guru*), and therefore he should be offered the best worship in this Vyāsa-pūjā. Although many exalted devotees are present here, no one can be equal or greater than Śrīla Prabhupāda, because everyones' very advancement originally comes from our dear Gurudeva. As an individual soul is the basic principle of the growth of one's material body, similarly Śrīla Prabhupāda is the principle of our spiritual growth. All kinds of Vedic ritualistic ceremonies are meant to bring us to the point of surrendering unto him. The goal of all *yoga* paths is to enable us to get his association. Dear Godbrothers, it is superfluous to speak about Śrīla Prabhupāda because every one of you know this intimate associate of Lord Śrī Kṛṣṇa, for whom there are no material and spiritual distinctions because he already lives in Goloka-Vṛndāvana Dhāma and sees everything here as the proprietorship of the Lord. Being the representative of Kṛṣṇa, he is to be considered as good as the Lord.

He is very expert in using everything in the service of Śrī Kṛṣṇa. By the grace of Śrīla Prabhupāda only, we are engaged in the practice of *bhakti,* which can be executed only with his mercy. Therefore he should receive this Vyāsa-pūjā offering, and no one should disagree. As by watering the root of the tree, the watering of the whole tree is automatically accomplished, so by offering this Vyāsa-pūjā to Śrīla Prabhupāda everyone will be satisfied. If anyone is intelligent, it will be very good for him to surrender to unto the lotus feet of Śrīla Prabhupāda. If we satisfy our dear *guru,* then automatically we satisfy Śrī Kṛṣṇa, and if we satisfy Śrī Kṛṣṇa, then we become satisfied.

All glories to Śrīla Prabhupāda,

*From the most fallen souls in your service*

Ādityeśa dāsa brahmacārī / Bhutabhavya dāsa brahmacārī / Brajendra-kumāra dāsa brahmacārī / Devyapati dāsa brahmacārī\* / Dvija-hari dāsa brahmacārī / Jaya Jagannātha dāsa brahmacārī / Kṛpālu dāsa brahmacārī / Kṣitimohana dāsa brahmacārī / Kutahanta dāsa brahmacārī / Madana monohara dāsa brahmacārī / Maitreya Muni dāsa brahmacārī / Rādhā-Kṛṣṇa dāsa brahmacārī / Stutya dāsa brahmacārī / Uddharaṇa dāsa brahmacārī / Viṣvaksena dāsa brahmacārī / Carudeha dāsa brahmacārī / Antonio / Enrique\* / Bhakta Eusebio / Bhakta Felipe 1 / Bhakta Felipe 2 / Bhakta Fermin / Bhakta Gerardo / Bhakta Guillermo / Bhakta Joaquin / Bhakta Gorge / Vinico / Bhakta Migues / Bhakta Nocolas / Bhakta Octavio / Bhakta Pascual / Nirlepa-devī dāsī / Rocana-devī dāsī / Bhaktin Adriana / Bhaktin Alicia / Bhaktin Carmen / Acaleśvara dāsa, Jaladoṣa-devī dāsī / Jagannātha Miśra dāsa, Jaganīya-devī dāsī, Govinda, Palika / Sadanava dāsa Bhaktin Tina / Saurasani-devī dāsī / Kṛṣṇa Caitanya / Guillermo Prabhu / Sergio Prabhu
